# Code map

Generated by `bun run codemap` from the first sentence of each source file's opening comment.
Do not edit by hand: change the file's comment and regenerate (the commit hook does it for you).
Tests are left out. For what a project word means, see [the glossary](../reference/glossary.md).

2967 files.

## scripts/audit/

- `page-audit.ts`: The in-page audit every walk runs on every visited state.
- `run.ts`: The audit launcher (bun).

## scripts/audit/walks/

- `clicks.ts`: RUN: clicks - the crawler.
- `editors.ts`: RUN: editors - one piece of every kind opened in its real editor (created through the ghost shelf's New button when the deck is empty, which audits the create flow too).
- `library.ts`: RUN: library - every deck x every view (both read from the live chip/switch rows), the staging bar, the delete confirm (dismissed), and the context menu.
- `promises.ts`: RUN: promises - buttons must DO what they say.
- `rooms.ts`: RUN: rooms - every app the dock and catalog offer, both themes.
- `setup.ts`: RUN: setup - the first-run wizard against a FRESH studio: every step (walked by pressing the wizard's own NEXT), the live theme flip, the custom accent tile, and the final screen.
- `shared.ts`: The shared walk rig (runs under NODE - bun's own CDP pipe hangs against chromium).

## scripts/

- `book-cover-catalog.ts`: Derive the cover catalog from drop-in asset folders; no hand-maintained design registry.
- `build-desktop.ts`: Build Bunraku.exe - real software from the same code the CLI runs.
- `build-pages.ts`: Build what GitHub Pages publishes: the website at the root and the browser-only Studio under studio/.
- `build-site.ts`: Build the public website into a folder: pages, the rendered user guides, media, fonts, style, and script.
- `capability-manifest.ts`: Generate the browser-safe capability import seam from entity and format drop-in folders.
- `code-map-core.ts`: The code map's pure half: which files it covers, each file's purpose (the first sentence of its opening documentation block, which scan:headers already requires), and the page itself.
- `code-map.ts`: Writes docs/generated/CODE-MAP.md, a one-line purpose for every source file (code-map-core.ts), so an agent can find where something lives with one search instead of guessing by file name.
- `docs-fields.ts`: Emit docs/generated/fields.<kind>.json from the live entity schemas (src/entities/<kind>/schema.ts).
- `docs-figures.ts`: Render the docs figure specs (docs/figures/*.js) to static SVGs, so the @fig figures become real assets the in-app Docs room and any markdown viewer can show.
- `docs-index.ts`: Emit the docs retrieval catalog from the docs corpus: docs/generated/docs-index.json, docs/llms.txt, and docs/generated/PAGE-INDEX.md.
- `docs-parts-core.ts`: The theme parts page's pure rules: read every `data-part` name out of a component source, and render the author-facing table.
- `docs-parts.ts`: Writes docs/generated/theme-parts.md, the stable `data-part` names a theme's styles can target, read from the component sources so no list is kept by hand.
- `docs-summaries-core.ts`: Pure core for the semantic-docs summary CLI: inventory, queue, status, paths, diagnostics.
- `docs-summaries-report.ts`: Inventory reporting and status-specific repair guidance for semantic docs.
- `docs-summaries.ts`: Non-networked authoring CLI for semantic documentation sidecars.
- `format-bake.ts`: Generate the static format registry required by compiled and browser-only builds.
- `format-matrix.ts`: Emit docs/FORMAT-SUPPORT.md from the live adapter registry.
- `helpers-core.ts`: Pure rules for the generated helpers index: the shared homes, exported names, and page shape.
- `helpers.ts`: Writes or checks HELPERS.md, an index of exported declarations in established shared homes.
- `ic-glyphs.ts`: Writes or checks src/generated/ic-glyphs.json, the path table behind `Ic`.
- `kit-manifest.ts`: Generate Kit's static drop-in manifest, so a compiled binary can find its own parts.
- `license-audit.ts`: Dependency license firewall: fail if a runtime/dev dep is AGPL, GPL, SSPL, or Elastic, or if a production dependency is not open source ("SEE LICENSE IN", UNLICENSED, all rights reserved).
- `macro-audit.ts`: Compare a hand-written macro catalog against what the engine itself says it has.
- `make-icon.ts`: Rasterize the bunny-b mark (a lowercase b whose stem is one ear, a leaf ear floating beside it, and a little ghost cut out of its face) into build/vaude.ico and build/vaude-256.png.
- `mutation-check.ts`: Runs the mutation check (mutation-core.ts over the list in mutations.ts): each guard in the risky folders is broken on purpose, one at a time, and its tests run; a break no test notices is a guard nothing protects.
- `mutation-core.ts`: The mutation check's pure half: turning a list of guards into mutants (small, deliberate breaks of the code) and applying them to source text.
- `mutations-cast.ts`: Mutants for the Cast wing's store guards: who a recast may change, the open-reply refusals, and the persona counters (the live-scene guard, the damaged-scene paths, the backfill's slices, its change check and its once...
- `mutations-theme-mods.ts`: Mutations for the theme mod guards (styles, fonts, images, slots, protected layer, lifecycle).
- `mutations.ts`: What the mutation check breaks (mutation-check.ts).
- `node-core-entry.ts`: Runtime smoke entry for the public core graph.
- `node-core-smoke.ts`: Build the public core import graph for Node and execute it with the installed Node runtime.
- `pages-preview-path.ts`: Resolve one preview request inside dist/pages on both Windows and POSIX hosts.
- `perf-browser.ts`: The browser half of the perf harness: seeds a throwaway studio with realistic data, boots the real dev server on it, drives it with a headless Chrome over CDP (playwright-core), and prints the section 11 matrix.
- `perf-count-cipher.ts`: Wraps a `CellCipher` to count `open()` calls by column ("table.column"), plus the bytes of the sealed cell each call opened.
- `perf-gate-assembly.ts`: The Send-time assembly budget: `assemble()` must not scale with a scene's whole stored history, only with what the budget can possibly keep.
- `perf-gate-data.ts`: Data builders for the perf harness (`perf-gate.ts`, `perf-browser.ts`).
- `perf-gate-health.ts`: The health check's speed budget: a scan opens every sealed cell, including a ~20 MB frozen snapshot, and must not hold the main thread past 100 ms, because the cell checks run on the background lane under a key lease...
- `perf-gate-lore.ts`: M3's own perf gate: "activation over 5,000 entries on a 30k-token scan text under 10 ms after warmup." Builds one synthetic 5,000-entry book (mixed substring/whole-word/case-sensitive/regex triggers, the same shape tr...
- `perf-gate-stats.ts`: The persona counters' speed budget: the one-time backfill of a scene that predates the counters must not hold the main thread past 100 ms, however big the scene is.
- `perf-gate-summaries.ts`: The R5 step 3.3 gate: "boot and the library listing open 0 documents.body_json cells".
- `perf-gate.ts`: The speed budget, enforced: nothing may block the main thread for more than 100 ms.
- `perf-measure.ts`: The perf gates' one stopwatch: runs `work` while a heartbeat timer records the longest time the event loop was held (the main thread's freeze), and how long the work took in all.
- `pin-sidecar.ts`: Pin the remote-access helper to a published release.
- `preview-pages.ts`: Serve the generated Pages artifact locally under the same /Bunraku/ project prefix.
- `probe-keychain.ts`: Runs the macOS Keychain or Linux Secret Service keystore backend against the REAL tool on this machine (CI's keychain job), because the unit tests only prove it against fakes.
- `probe-sqlite-runtime.ts`: Proves the SQLite runtime capabilities the data model depends on, inside whatever artifact is running this code.
- `publish-export-core.ts`: The pure half of `bun run publish:export`: which tracked paths stay private, and which text in the public remainder still points at one of them (a markdown link, a "see ..." note, the name of a private file).
- `publish-export.ts`: Writes a clean copy of the tracked tree, minus the paths in publish-private.txt, into a new folder, then fails if any file left in the copy still points at a private path.
- `risu-macros.ts`: Generate src/core/preset/macros/risu.ts from RisuAI's own CBS documentation.
- `sidecar-pin-format.ts`: Reading a release's SHA256SUMS into helper pins, and writing the committed pins module.
- `sillytavern-macros.ts`: Generate src/core/preset/macros/sillytavern-new.ts from SillyTavern's registry engine.
- `test-react-dom.ts`: Loads react-dom once, before any test file, with a DOM present (bunfig.toml preload).
- `test-sandbox.ts`: The test sandbox, preloaded before every `bun test` run (bunfig.toml): every place Bunraku keeps per-user files points at one throwaway folder, so no test can write into the person's real home or read their real crede...
- `theme-measure.ts`: theme:measure - what share of the WHOLE app looks structurally different across the four bundled themes.
- `third-party-notices.ts`: Writes or checks THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md: name, version, license and repository of every package in the production dependency closure (package.json `dependencies`, followed through each installed package's own `depende...
- `ui-bundle.ts`: Shared Bun browser-bundle helpers for the desktop bake and static Pages build.
- `update-macro-pins.ts`: Regenerate hermetic macro-catalog source pins after an intentional upstream review.
- `vaude-browser.ts`: Open Bunraku in the default browser (hot-reloading).
- `wireframe-theme.ts`: Regenerate the palette block in design/wireframes/theme.js from the bundled theme manifests, so wireframes that switch themes by data-theme paint the same custom properties the app's painter writes.

## scripts/hooks/

- `apps-documented.ts`: Every app a person can open has a section in the UI reference.
- `catalog-lib.ts`: Pure extraction logic for the component catalog (component-catalog.ts's functional core).

- `file-selection.ts`: Pure source extensions and distinct skip-directory policies shared by repository file walkers.
- `gate.ts`: The structural + discipline gate: the imperative shell around lib.ts.

- `single-source-storage-protocol.ts`: Exact retained temporary ciphertext, scratch, and artifact write protocols for the storage guard.
- `single-source-syntax.ts`: TypeScript syntax queries for shared-helper implementations, excluding inert text and imports.
- `single-source-ui.ts`: Single-source guards for the UI folds of the 2026-10 DRY audit (section D).
- `stage-boundary-core.ts`: The D18 boundary lint, as pure functions over a path and its source text.
- `stage-boundary.ts`: Runs the D18 boundary lint over every authored TypeScript file under src/ and scripts/.
- `style-guard-core.ts`: Style guard detectors (pure): a UI line that names a font instead of using the theme's font tokens, sizes a pane by the viewport instead of its container (AGENTS.md rule 11), or styles the button element outside the b...
- `style-guard.ts`: Style guard - fonts come from the theme, panes size by container.
- `theme-keys-allowlist.ts`: Files still keying on a theme id.
- `theme-keys-core.ts`: Pure detector for theme-keys: host CSS or TS that selects on a theme's id (`html[data-theme="theater"]`) instead of a surface pick.

## src/

- `app-data.ts`: Where Bunraku keeps MACHINE-LOCAL support files - one owner, so two features cannot disagree.
- `browser-mode.ts`: Detect the static, memory-only browser Studio and announce committed receipt changes.
- `cli-derived.ts`: `bunraku derived --stats|--clear`: inspect or discard only rebuildable derived rows.
- `cli-export.ts`: `bunraku export <folder> [studio]`: the library written out as a plain studio folder (src/studio/folder-export.ts).
- `cli-inspect.ts`: `inspect`'s per-kind summary: the few lines worth printing about each canonical kind.
- `cli-io.ts`: Pure CLI convert IO policy: flag parse, path identity, container agreement, atomic publish.
- `cli-validation.ts`: CLI validation boundary.
- `cli.ts`: bunraku - the Bunraku command line.
- `color.ts`: Shared hex/RGB/HSV color contract: parse once, convert, and format the lowercase six-digit CSS color Studio uses.
- `convert.ts`: The bundle/link layer that sits above the single-entity adapters: a card is not just a character, it may bundle an embedded lorebook.
- `desktop-boot-report.ts`: Boot-failure reporting for the packaged desktop app - the surface that makes a failed launch SAY something.
- `desktop-dev.ts`: Bunraku (dev) - the native desktop window over the LIVE source.
- `desktop-focus.ts`: Second launch: bring the running Bunraku window forward instead of opening another onto the same library (decision 9).
- `desktop-port.ts`: Desktop listen-port selection and claim policy.
- `desktop-window-launch.ts`: Shared window-worker wiring; each caller supplies its compile-safe worker factory and its own failure policy.
- `desktop-window.ts`: The native window, in a WORKER thread - because webview's run() is a blocking native message pump that would starve the server's event loop on the main thread (verified live: window up, server dead).
- `desktop.ts`: Bunraku.exe - the desktop entry.
- `ensure-formats.ts`: One owner for "make the format registry ready in whatever runtime this is".
- `error-message.ts`: Render an error's message, a caller fallback, or the value's string form in that order.
- `foreign-reader.ts`: Reading a file in the studio folder that is not one of ours.
- `version.ts`: The one version constant the CLI banner, the server, and the update check all read.

## src/core/lore/

- `_test-helpers.ts`: Shared factories for lore core tests.
- `book-ops.ts`: Pure lorebook split / merge / duplicate / renumber.

- `empty-book.ts`: Deterministic empty lorebook body factory for "New lorebook".

- `heal.ts`: Silent lorebook heal for import staging.
- `health.ts`: Soft lore authoring diagnostics.
- `index.ts`: Pure lore capabilities, summaries, health, empty factory, sample match, plus inspect and diff, and the one lore engine chat and the Workbench share (engine/).
- `inspect.ts`: Nine-rule lorebook linter.
- `near-miss.ts`: Near-miss keyword suggester for Playback (prototype LogAnalyzerView Levenshtein).
- `platform-fields.ts`: Which first-class lore fields each Write-for profile OWNS on a real wire.
- `rng.ts`: Injected RNG helpers for activation / Rehearsal.
- `sample-match.ts`: Thin authoring-aid key match preview.
- `summary.ts`: Deterministic lorebook chips for Library/Press (pure, no mutation).
- `web-layout.ts`: Pure force-directed layout tick for the Web lens.

## src/core/lore/engine/

- `_test-helpers.ts`: Shared factories for engine/*.test.ts.
- `boosting.ts`: Cross-entry boosting (RoleCall selection/boosting.ts applyProbabilityBoosts, engine.ts step 6): after inclusion groups and before the budget, every entry that fired adds its `boostAmount` to the chance of each entry i...
- `budget.ts`: Priority/sortOrder eviction and two-stage budgeting (Bunraku change 3, - do NOT copy RoleCall's selection/priority.ts here).
- `character-filter.ts`: Whitelist/blacklist an entry to specific characters by name or tag.
- `engine.ts`: The lore engine orchestrator: RoleCall's engine.ts process(), restructured as a pure function over canonical books plus Bunraku's four changes (derived timers, seeded rng, sortOrder/priority budgeting, positions retur...
- `groups.ts`: Inclusion groups: when several triggered entries share a `groupName`, only one injects, picked by weighted random draw (`groupWeight`).
- `index.ts`: Public surface of the lore engine.
- `key-index.ts`: M3, compiled lore matcher: a multi-pattern matcher over a book's plain (non-regex, non-special) trigger keys, so one pass over an entry's scan text finds every plain key hit, instead of triggers-keyword.ts's matchKeyw...
- `key-word-boundary.ts`: The one definition of a "word character" for whole-word lore key matching, shared by triggers-keyword.ts (regex lookarounds) and key-index.ts (per-character check) so the two paths can never disagree.
- `outlet.ts`: {{lorebook:EntryName}} outlet expansion.
- `recursion.ts`: Recursive trigger matching: an entry's own content can trigger other entries.
- `scan.ts`: Build an entry's scan text and evaluate its triggers.
- `side-effects.ts`: Declarative variable mutations for injecting entries.
- `stored-input.ts`: Private revision trust for one deeply immutable body returned by the encrypted store.
- `timers.ts`: Derived timer state (Bunraku change 1: timers are derived, never stored).
- `triggers-emotion-banks-1.ts`: Emotion pattern data, part 1 of 2: primary + secondary emotions.
- `triggers-emotion-banks-2.ts`: Emotion pattern data, part 2 of 2: complex + physical/situational emotions.
- `triggers-emotion.ts`: [emotion:X] trigger matching.
- `triggers-keyword.ts`: Plain-text and regex keyword matching.
- `triggers-special.ts`: Special contextual triggers ([messageCount:X], [speaker:X], [recency:Xm], ...).
- `types.ts`: Public API types for the lore engine plus the internal book+entry pairing shared across engine/*.ts.
- `wake.ts`: Recursion wake graph: which entries can wake which via recursion, computed with the engine's own keyword matcher.

## src/core/macros/

- `catalog.ts`: Every macro family, in RoleCall's registration order.
- `choice-groups.ts`: Choice groups: a preset declares select_one, select_many, toggle, or input sections that render as real controls instead of banks of fake toggle prompts.
- `conditions.ts`: Condition strings ("A != B", "x >= 3", "!flag", bare truthiness), as RoleCall evaluates them for block-if and for the iteration and conditional macro families ({{filter}}, {{when_all}}).
- `evaluate.ts`: The tree-walking evaluator: RoleCall's handler inside ADR-012.
- `index.ts`: The macro engine: RoleCall's, inside ADR-012's closed world.
- `inspector.ts`: The State inspector's helpers (RoleCall `packages/macros/src/inspector.ts`, ported for the Context wing's State tab): a stored variable key read back as the scope its author wrote, and a value as text.
- `lazy.ts`: Raw lazy-argument operations shared by macros that preserve authored whitespace and separators.
- `limits.ts`: The engine's bounds (ADR-012 "Constraints on the evaluator"), in one place so handlers that build strings ({{repeat}}, {{pad}}) can refuse an oversized result before allocating it, not after.
- `lists.ts`: Macros pass lists to each other as JSON-array strings ({{enabled_list}} returns one; {{foreach}}, {{join}}, and {{count}} accept one).
- `normalize.ts`: Input normalization ahead of the macro parser: RoleCall's `preprocessSyntax`, rule for rule and in its order, WITH an offset map back to the authored text.
- `parse.ts`: The macro parser: a span-carrying port of RoleCall's clean-room `parseNodesV2`.
- `phases.ts`: Evaluation phases (RoleCall's macro engine spec Part VIII; RoleCall's macros/phases.ts).
- `process.ts`: The engine's entry point: normalize, parse, evaluate, post-process (RoleCall's `processMacros`), and report the render as top-level segments tied to the authored source (Bunraku's addition, for the preset workbench's...
- `registry.ts`: The closed macro registry (ADR-012 decision 5), holding RoleCall's handler families.
- `scopes.ts`: Variable scopes: `scope:key` ({{state.set::session:callsign::X}}) resolved onto the two storage maps, as RoleCall resolves them.
- `types.ts`: The macro engine's shared types: RoleCall's engine inside ADR-012.

## src/core/macros/handlers/

- `character.ts`: Character card macros: fields carried on the card beyond identity (scenario, examples, tags, creator metadata, colors).
- `choice.ts`: Choice macros: read the preset's choice-group selections (choice-groups.ts) at render time, so prompts can react to what the user picked in the preset UI without toggle-prompt gymnastics.
- `composition.ts`: Composition and template macros (macro engine spec Part V.1 / V.2).
- `conditional.ts`: Conditional macros: if/else, switch, comparison operators, and the plain-English shortcuts ({{when_all}}, {{when_any}}, {{whenempty}}, {{ifset}}).
- `identity.ts`: Identity macros: character, user, and model identity.
- `in-reply.ts`: In-reply ask macros: RoleCall's handler inside ADR-012.
- `iteration.ts`: Iteration and list macros.
- `lorebook.ts`: Lorebook macros: entry lookup and stats over what the lorebook engine triggered this run.
- `lumiverse-compat.ts`: Lumiverse compatibility macros: macros from Lumiverse's preset dialect so imported presets (e.g. ThreadBare) run natively: a running counter, reasoning-tag emitters, group-card macros, and platform tokens.
- `math.ts`: {{calc::expr}} - safe arithmetic over already-expanded values, so derived numbers (e.g. a countdown, `floor - since_start`) can be displayed without mutating any variable.
- `phase-macros.ts`: Phase macro fallbacks: RoleCall's handler inside ADR-012.
- `preset-introspection.ts`: Preset toggle-introspection macros: "which prompts are toggled on?" as a first-class primitive, so a roster section can read the live prompt list at render time instead of registering setvars or counting separators by...
- `pronouns.ts`: Pronoun macros: gender-aware pronouns for the character and the user.
- `random.ts`: Random and dice macros: RoleCall's handler inside ADR-012.
- `roleplay.ts`: Roleplay convenience macros: shorthand for `{{getvar::x}}` on a handful of common story variables (mood, relationship, location, ...), so lorebook side effects and preset authors don't have to spell out `{{getvar::moo...
- `runtime.ts`: Runtime/environment macros: RoleCall's handler inside ADR-012.
- `scene.ts`: Chat-context macros: the running conversation (RoleCall's own file name for this family is "scene", despite the content being message/chat access, not stage blocking).
- `st-compat-vars.ts`: ST variable-macro compat shim: RoleCall's handler, inside ADR-012.
- `state.ts`: The state.* namespace: RoleCall's handler inside ADR-012.
- `stats.ts`: Stats and meta macros: RoleCall's handler inside ADR-012.

## src/extensions/

- `slot-registry.ts`: The fixed list of named slots a theme package may fill, and the one parser for what goes in them.
- `surface-registry.ts`: The host's surface and variant ids: the one list a theme manifest is validated against.

- `theme-id.ts`: The bounded imported theme package ID accepted at package and saved-setting boundaries.

## src/kit/

## src/kit/commands/

- `doctor.ts`: /doctor: run the read-only, bounded diagnostic playbill.
- `gallery.ts`: /gallery: your card art along the bottom of the screen.
- `gates.ts`: /gates: how often Kit asks before it writes.

- `share.ts`: /share: point Kit at a folder it may READ.

- `tools.ts`: /tools: open the human capability browser without exposing more tools to the model.
- `unshare.ts`: /unshare: stop Kit reading a folder.
- `usage.ts`: /usage: how much of a connected subscription is gone, straight from the provider.

## src/kit/keystore/

- `aes.ts`: The authenticated-encryption primitive every key-holding backend shares.
- `keystore.ts`: Keystore: seal and open secrets.
- `registry.ts`: Keystore registry: the known backends in seal-priority order, plus lookup by tag.
- `types.ts`: Keystore types: the sealed-blob envelope and the backend contract.

## src/kit/keystore/backends/

- `dpapi.ts`: DPAPI keystore backend (Windows): encrypts at rest with the user's login secret via ProtectedData (CurrentUser scope), so the vault is useless on any other machine or account and needs no passphrase.
- `identity-file.ts`: Identity-file keystore backend: a key Kit generates for itself, so a machine with no OS keystore still gets an encrypted vault instead of no vault at all.
- `identity-write.ts`: Exclusive identity-file creation with cleanup for a write that fails partway through.
- `keychain-key.ts`: The shape both OS-keychain backends share (macOS Keychain, Linux Secret Service): one random 256-bit key lives in the operating system's secret store, and every seal is AES-256-GCM under it (the shared aes.ts).
- `linux-secret.ts`: Linux Secret Service keystore backend: the vault key, and the marker that one was made, live in the desktop keyring (GNOME Keyring, KWallet, KeePassXC) through `secret-tool` from libsecret.
- `macos-keychain.ts`: macOS Keychain keystore backend: the vault key, and the marker that one was made, live in the login keychain as generic password items, through Apple's `security` tool.
- `passphrase.ts`: Passphrase keystore backend: portable, dependency-free encryption at rest.
- `spawn-collect.ts`: Collect one child process's output while preserving caller-selected text encodings.

## src/kit/loop/_shared/

- `test-harness.ts`: Shared harness for the loop tests: drive runTurn with scripted replies and a harmless dispatcher.

## src/kit/loop/

- `dispatch.ts`: Dispatch: the bridge between the model's tool calls and our tools.
- `loop-core.ts`: The pure ReAct core: drive one user turn by calling the injected model, dispatching the tool calls it asks for, feeding results back, and repeating until it answers or a stop condition trips.
- `scheduler.ts`: Explicit tool-call scheduler: read-only batches may overlap while any draft, apply, or unknown effect makes the complete provider batch execute serially.
- `state.ts`: Pure lifecycle reducer for one Kit model/tool turn.
- `stop-core.ts`: Pure stop conditions for the agent loop: when to end a turn regardless of what the model wants.

- `plan-usage.ts`: What a turn has cost against a subscription plan, from the two services that will say.
- `probe.ts`: Provider probe: the proof-of-life behind /test (RC parity).
- `provider.ts`: The provider seam: the one vendor-agnostic shape the loop calls a model through (hub-and-spoke).
- `registry.ts`: The provider registry: the impure loader for spokes/, mirroring core/loader.ts and tools/discover.ts.
- `scene.ts`: The provider a scene sends through (items 8 and 9): a saved Kit connection, turned into the stage's OpenAI-compatible provider.
- `spoke.ts`: The one shape a provider is (hub-and-spoke).

- `usage.ts`: The canonical token-usage shape and its tolerant combinators: the inner-layer domain type the meter and tally both read, plus the boundary reader that turns a provider's ragged usage numbers into a clean TokenUsage.
- `vault.ts`: Encrypted vault: the saved providers, sealed at rest by the keystore.

- `persona-create.ts`: Persona creation workflow preserving the brief versus injected-content boundary.

- `rail-open.ts`: `/rail`, from Kit's side.
- `read.ts`: Read tool: open one piece and hand its canonical content to the model.

- `search.ts`: Read tool: find pieces whose name, id, or kind contains a query.
- `studio-art.ts`: `studio_art`: put a piece's own card art on screen.
- `tool.ts`: The one canonical tool shape for Kit (hub-and-spoke).
- `transfer.ts`: Studio transfer, preview half: serialize one stored piece through another platform's adapter and report what the crossing costs, without writing anything.

- `chat-store.contract-stats.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite's persona counters: RoleCall's persona stats kept as running counters, updated by the same write they count.
- `chat-store.contract-summary.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite's summary half: a scene's summary settings, and one summary text per take.
- `chat-store.contract-swipe-cuts.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite's removed versions: a swipe cut from its message is soft, restorable from `swipeGraveyard`, and invisible to every read, index and prompt until it is back.
- `chat-store.contract-trackers.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite's trackers half: what a scene's tracker Props remember, one entry per module.
- `chat-store.contract-trees.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite's tree half: a book's stored tree layout is kept beside the scene, the newest live one is current, Undo marks it undone and brings the one before it back, and nothing is ever deleted.
- `chat-store.contract-turns.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite's turn half: checkpoints that stream without bumping anything, the one-transaction commit that stores a reply with its trace, a branch's macro variables, and the connection and model a sce...
- `chat-store.contract-variant.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite's variant half: a scene remembers which of its character's alt-art variants it plays.
- `chat-store.contract-writes.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite's write-safety half: stale writes (edge case 9) and soft delete (D23).
- `chat-store.contract.ts`: The ChatStore contract suite, first pass.
- `chat-store.ts`: The ChatStore contract: chats, branches, forking, messages, swipes, edits, bounded transcript reads, and selected-only swipe reads; then checkpoints, stored traces, macro variables, the scene's connection, the scene's...
- `chat-summary-types.ts`: What a scene keeps of its chat summary (RoleCall's `summary_prompts` settings and `user_chats.summary`): the settings that govern it, scene-wide, and one text per take.
- `chat-tracker-types.ts`: What a scene keeps of its trackers: RoleCall's legacy tracker modules (Stats, Inventory, ...), one entry per module.
- `chat-tree-types.ts`: A stored Compendium tree layout: the tree a model built from a book's entries, kept with the scene beside the book and never inside it (04b F3).
- `content-store-asset.ts`: Bounded opaque asset reads and the targeted portrait reference pinned to a piece version.
- `content-store-blobs.contract.ts`: The ContentStore blob contract: one sealed row per content hash, exact reference rows, deletion with the last ref, across BOTH backends.
- `content-store-concurrency.contract.ts`: The ContentStore concurrency contract: whichever caller wins a race, the loser never corrupts the winner.
- `content-store-light.ts`: Explicit partial entity envelope used only by light editor reads.
- `content-store-parts.contract.ts`: Appendix A P2's own contract: a one-block edit writes exactly one part and drops none, an untouched escrow seals no `r/` part, prune keeps every surviving version byte-identical, and purge/bury/unbury round-trip exactly.
- `content-store-patch-lorebook.contract.ts`: The patch path contract's lorebook leg, beside `content-store-patch.contract.ts`'s preset leg: a different `KindParts` registration (`entries`, not `prompts`), a different item schema, and a body shape with extra fiel...
- `content-store-patch.contract.ts`: The patch path contract: run by `content-store.contract.ts` for every backend.
- `content-store-summary.ts`: Public listing shapes derived from one live entity and its lightweight shelf facts.
- `content-store.contract-fixtures.ts`: Shared pieces of the ContentStore contract suites (content-store.contract.ts and content-store-concurrency.contract.ts): a character, a planted row, and the rig every test runs on.
- `content-store.contract.ts`: The ContentStore contract suite.
- `content-store.ts`: The ContentStore contract: how Bunraku reads and writes library content, and the dumb DocumentBackend it is built on.
- `director-types.ts`: What the Story Director keeps (RoleCall's `user_chats.dm_context` and the `dm*` chat settings, trimmed to the fields something writes).
- `document-order.ts`: Stable document order shared by content-store policy and opaque backend listings.
- `piece-change-contract.ts`: Backend wire shapes for row identities and ordered committed piece-change receipts.
- `piece-patch.ts`: Send-only-changed editor patch contract shared by ContentStore and its HTTP adapter.
- `pipeline.ts`: The prompt pipeline's hook contract (Decision 3).
- `provider.ts`: The Provider contract (Decision 2): the one request, reply, stream, and error shape every model call uses.
- `state-store.ts`: The StateStore contract: the Studio's own documents, one JSON text per key, kept in the library beside its pieces.

## src/stage/crypto/

- `cell-cipher.ts`: What an encrypting store needs from the keyring: seal and open one cell, and hash a value the database searches by.

- `device-vault.ts`: The keyring's device vault in the app: Kit's key vault (src/kit/keystore), which seals with the strongest lock this computer has (DPAPI, the macOS Keychain, the Linux keyring, or the identity-file fallback) and opens...

- `password-worker.ts`: The password lock's worker on the background lane (src/stage/worker-lane.ts): Argon2id runs here, off the main thread, so checking a password never pauses the app.

- `breather.ts`: The speed budget inside long loops (Performance): nothing may hold the main thread past 100 ms.

- `chat-store.ts`: Runtime-neutral memory ChatStore composition for tests and the browser-only Stage.

- `content-store-copy.ts`: Duplicating a piece by reference: the copy's parts are written from the source's own cached-style fill, so every picture stays a link to its existing blob and nothing is expanded, re-hashed or resealed.
- `content-store-history.ts`: History reads and restores, pinned to the live row's monotonic write identity.
- `content-store-hold.ts`: The patch path's cache fill for a backend with no cold-read worker (the memory backend, `:memory:`): the same "keep pictures as references" fill the SQLite worker does, built from the backend's light reads.

- `content-store-media-refs.ts`: What an editor patch may put into an art or blob reference slot (one concept, split from content-store-patch.ts for the line cap).
- `content-store-patch.ts`: `ContentStore.patch`: the send-only-what-changed save.
- `content-store-read.ts`: One whole-entity read with writeId retries and the optional SQLite cold-worker owner.
- `content-store-stable-read.ts`: Stable live-row decoding and the summary rebuild callback, shared by ContentStore read owners.
- `content-store-write.ts`: The ContentStore's write path via parts.
- `content-store.ts`: createContentStore: the one ContentStore implementation.
- `continue-rules.ts`: The store's rule for what a continue leaves on a swipe's generation record (ChatStore rule 31).

- `fake-snapshot-rig.ts`: Test support for the snapshot suites: a real encrypted library on a real file (fake key vault and fast password lock, as the keyring tests use), with a snapshot store beside it.
- `final-cut-rules.ts`: The input check and the merge for a scene's Final Cut, applied by every ChatStore implementation so they cannot refuse or keep different things.
- `generation-rules.ts`: The check for how a generated swipe was made (RoleCall's SwipeData generation fields): plain data only, every field its own type, an unknown field refused.
- `opening-rules.ts`: The input check for a scene's edited opening messages, applied by every ChatStore implementation.
- `participant-rules.ts`: The input check and the change for a scene's participant roster, applied by every ChatStore implementation so they cannot refuse or keep different things.
- `persona-stats-rules.ts`: What each persona's running counters hold and how they read, shared by both ChatStore backends.
- `piece-cache.ts`: The patch path's write-side cache: a bounded, process-local memo of a piece's last decompose, so a warm patch never has to fetch and hash the live parts a whole-entity save would.
- `piece-change-replay.ts`: Serializes bounded journal replay for derived owners that follow committed document changes.

- `prompting-rules.ts`: The input check and the merge for a scene's guides and scene note, applied by every ChatStore implementation so they cannot refuse or keep different things.

- `summary-rules.ts`: The input checks and the merge for a scene's summary, applied by every ChatStore implementation so they cannot refuse or keep different things.
- `swipe-cut-rules.ts`: Rule 32's two small decisions, shared by both ChatStore backends.
- `tracker-rules.ts`: The input check and the merge for a scene's trackers, applied by every ChatStore implementation so they cannot refuse or keep different things.
- `transcript-search-rules.ts`: The transcript search's query rule and match rule, shared by the memory and SQLite stores.
- `tree-rules.ts`: The input check for a stored tree layout, applied by every ChatStore implementation so they cannot keep different things.

- `migration-33-backdrop.ts`: Migration 33: a scene's backdrop (ChatStore rule 36).
- `migration-34-compendium.ts`: Migration 34: a scene's Compendium choices (ChatStore rule 38).
- `migration-35-receipts.ts`: Migration 35: Compendium receipts (ChatStore rule 39).
- `migration-36-trees.ts`: Migration 36: stored Compendium tree layouts (ChatStore rule 40).
- `migration-37-participants.ts`: Migration 37: a scene's participant roster (ChatStore rule 41).
- `migration-38-scene-regex.ts`: Migration 38: a scene's regex sets (ChatStore rule 42).
- `migration-39-trackers.ts`: Migration 39: what a scene's tracker Props remember (ChatStore rule 43).
- `migration-40-final-cut.ts`: Migration 40: what a scene keeps of Final Cut (ChatStore rule 44).
- `migration-41-scene-summary.ts`: Migration 41: what a scene keeps of its chat summary (ChatStore rule 45).
- `migration-42-director.ts`: Migration 42: the Story Director (ChatStore rule 47).
- `migrations.ts`: The database's migrations, in order.
- `piece-change-journal.ts`: Read and append the bounded sealed journal used by the per-piece change stream.
- `piece-parts-sql.ts`: SQLite storage for `piece_parts` and `blob_refs`.
- `quarantine.ts`: The SQLite stores quarantine only errors that mean persisted row data cannot be decoded.
- `row-fingerprints.ts`: Row fingerprints: a keyed hash of each covered row, kept in `row_fingerprints` and re-made inside the transaction that changed the row.
- `snapshot-jobs.ts`: The snapshot jobs the background lane runs on its own connection (maintenance-worker.ts): write a clean copy of the library, compare a snapshot with the library as it is now, and restore one in a single transaction.
- `snapshot-retention.ts`: Which snapshots survive: the newest one in each of the last 24 hours, 7 days, and 4 weeks that have one, plus the last few made just before a restore.
- `snapshots.ts`: Rolling snapshots of the library file and whole-library restore.
- `state-store.ts`: The SQLite StateStore: the Studio's own documents in `studio_state` (migration 7), one row per key, the text a sealed cell.
- `swipe-sql.ts`: The SQL the SQLite ChatStore writes about a message's versions, in one place: which swipes are live, where the next one goes, and how a message points at its current one.
- `test-support.ts`: Shared SQLite test harness: a fresh cell cipher and a fresh in-memory StageDatabase, hand-copied into 9 `.sqlite.test.ts`/`.regressions.test.ts` files before this fold.

- `render-markup.ts`: Markup renderer - turns a stored field string into a sanitized HTML string safe for dangerouslySetInnerHTML.
- `render-policy.ts`: Render policy - the PURE, DOM-free half of the markup renderer.
- `sanitize-svg.ts`: Sanitize a drop-in app's untrusted mark SVG.
- `scene-art.tsx`: Visibility-gated scene art; frozen snapshots stay immutable and legacy scenes follow committed live receipts.
- `scene-handoff.ts`: Hand-offs between apps: one app leaves a value, another reads it on mount and on the event.
- `session-marker.ts`: The one string that means "your page is older than this server".
- `studio-patch-request.ts`: Runtime-neutral validation of editor patch envelopes for desktop and temporary browser Studio.
- `studio-read-route.ts`: Runtime-neutral Studio read routes, shared by authenticated desktop routing and temporary browser Studio.
- `surface-variant.ts`: Which variant of a host surface the verified theme selected.
- `svg-safety.ts`: Validates the deliberately small, inert SVG subset accepted for staged NamedAssets.
- `switch-decision.ts`: Pure decisions about MOVING between versions (distinct from reading history, which is version-history.ts): which direction a switch goes, and whether it crosses a release that changed how the studio is stored.
- `test-dom.ts`: One JSDOM bootstrap for every src/ui test file instead of each file retyping its own `new JSDOM(...)` + `Object.assign(globalThis, {...})` + restore-on-teardown wiring (48 copies, dup-audit/ui.md item 4).
- `theme-slot-core.ts`: What a theme's slots hold, read once from the root attribute the painter writes (`data-theme-slots`).
- `theme-slot.tsx`: <ThemeSlot id>: the host-drawn home for a theme's static content.
- `toast.tsx`: The one bottom-of-scene action toast: a message and one action button ("Redo", "Undo").
- `typing-target.ts`: Is somebody typing into this?
- `ui-variant-styles.ts`: The shared ui.* surface-variant stylesheets (panel, meter, toast, empty, toolbar, section-header, tabs, label-type), imported once by the shell for their side effect.
- `update-check.ts`: Update check, the honest kind: nothing here fires on its own.
- `update-fetch.ts`: Common timeout and headers for the two fixed-host GitHub release requests.
- `use-copy-feedback.ts`: Copy feedback for one piece of text (RoleCall useMessageActions): copied or failed for 1.5 s.
- `use-debounced-value.ts`: Keep a value current only after it has stayed unchanged for the requested delay (useDebouncedValue), or save one after it rests (useDebouncedSave).
- `use-escape-key.ts`: One Escape arbiter for document-level consumers and modal owners.
- `use-flash.ts`: The timed feedback hooks (dry audit B5): a value that shows for a moment and clears itself ("Copied", a notice), and a toast that dismisses itself a while after it appears.
- `use-scoped-accents.ts`: The scoped accent properties for a container, from the piece colors it was given and the theme painted on the root.
- `use-visible-interval.ts`: A `setInterval` that stops paying for itself the moment nobody could see its result.
- `user-fonts.ts`: Loads a user font from bounded in-memory asset bytes; never evaluates a user URL or CSS source.
- `variant-css-check.ts`: The proofs every variant stylesheet owes, in one place so scenes, settings, editors and controls cannot drift: each rule sits under its own root attribute, corners stay square, colors come from tokens, focus stays vis...
- `variant-css.ts`: Variant stylesheets are plain CSS strings keyed on the root attribute the painter sets (`html[data-surface-scene-layout="vn-stage"]`), so a variant a theme does not pick matches nothing and the surface keeps its curre...
- `version-history.ts`: Version-history pure core: turn GitHub's /releases payload into the in-app timeline the Updates page renders (how far behind you are, every release's commits, and per-row update/rollback direction).
- `web-storage.ts`: Select browser storage, with an explicitly volatile implementation for the static Studio.
- `while-busy.ts`: Retry a studio read the server refused only because the library was busy ("studio_busy", HTTP 503).
- `window-memory.ts`: What this browser window remembers between reloads, and how to make it forget.

## src/ui/_shared/variants/ui.controls/

- `game.ts`: ui.controls game (Encore): two-pixel frames, a hard edge under each key, mono capitals on the buttons, a bold focus ring.
- `ink.ts`: ui.controls ink (Paper): hairline frames, fields as a ruled underline, small capitals on the Stamp, a dotted focus ring.
- `quiet.ts`: ui.controls quiet (Theater): hairline frames, no offset shadow, a thin focus ring; the shell Stamp lies flat.
- `sticker.ts`: ui.controls sticker (GirliePop): thick slab frames and a stamp shadow on every field and button, friendly bold labels, a chunky focus ring.

## src/ui/_shared/virtual-list/

- `core.tsx`: The one virtualizer every long list in the Studio reuses: today the Library's Cards/Tiles/List/Trash views (variable column count, read live from the browser's own CSS grid layout), later the scene transcript (variabl...
- `index.ts`: The Studio's one virtualizer module: `useVirtualRows` and its focus, Tab and grid helpers (core.tsx), the windowed flat list `VirtualRows` (rows.tsx) and the windowed card grid `VirtualShelf` (shelf.tsx).
- `rows.tsx`: A windowed flat list for rows of any height.
- `shelf.tsx`: The windowed card grid shared by the Shelf and Sets views: rows of N auto-fill cards inside a scroller that also holds a search bar above and a drop target below, so the rows are offset by `scrollMargin`.

## src/ui/agent/

- `agent-ledger.ts`: What has left this machine from the window, so `/privacy` can answer honestly.
- `command-context.ts`: The window's CommandContext: every shell action a Kit command may take, RECORDED rather than done.
- `command-core.ts`: The wire between Kit's slash commands and the window: what a command IS, and what running one DID.
- `command-sessions.ts`: Kit's saved sessions, from the window: `/session`, `/resume`, `/rewind`, `/export`.
- `compact-history.ts`: Compacting a conversation before the turn that would not have fitted.
- `compaction-core.ts`: Deciding when a conversation is too long, and what to do about it.
- `dock-position.ts`: Where the floating agent panel sits, and how it survives a window that changed size.
- `floating-agent.tsx`: The agent, floating over whatever you are actually doing.
- `gate-answer.ts`: Reading a Gate answer off the wire.
- `gate-card.tsx`: The Gate, in a browser.
- `html-handoff.ts`: Hand one HTML document from the transcript to the viewer app, and ask the shell to open it.
- `image-data.ts`: Bytes into something an `img` tag can show, once, at the boundary.
- `kit-bands-core.ts`: The arithmetic behind Kit's transcript bands: what folds, what a folded line says, and how much of a failure is allowed on screen.
- `kit-bands.tsx`: Kit's transcript bands: the copyable one, the folded one, the failed one, and the toast.
- `kit-choice-core.ts`: Reading a `ask_choice` payload, once, at the boundary.
- `kit-choice.tsx`: A question with options, as a panel you answer.
- `kit-command-bands.tsx`: What a slash command drew: a listing, a diagnostic, or a picture.
- `kit-command-style.ts`: The stylesheet for the slash-command surfaces: the listing, the doctor, the shelf, and the popup.
- `kit-markdown-core.ts`: The rules Kit's transcript markdown obeys, restated for a surface that has a DOM.
- `kit-markdown.tsx`: A reply, rendered the way Kit renders one.
- `kit-meters-core.ts`: The meters' arithmetic: Kit's, imported, plus the two facts this window has to decide for itself.
- `kit-meters.tsx`: Kit's meter row: how full the context is on the left, what the tokens cost on the right.
- `kit-transcript-style.ts`: The stylesheet for the transcript widgets, in Kit's own visual language.
- `kit-transcript.tsx`: The transcript itself: every line, in the band its role earns it.
- `kit-vars.ts`: Kit's palette, as CSS custom properties.
- `kit-widgets-core.ts`: The arithmetic behind Kit's live transcript widgets, ported exactly.
- `kit-widgets.tsx`: Kit's live transcript widgets, in the window.
- `live-state.ts`: Who holds the snapshot of the screen the user was last on.
- `open-piece.ts`: Taking a piece named in the conversation and putting it on the bench.
- `pending-gates.ts`: Questions the agent is holding, waiting for somebody to answer in a browser.
- `routes.ts`: The agent window's routes, in one place.
- `server-agent.ts`: The agent window's line to a model.
- `server-commands.ts`: Kit's slash commands, reachable from the window.
- `server-providers.ts`: Managing which models this studio can reach, from the window.
- `slash-core.ts`: What the composer is asking for while somebody types a slash line.
- `slash-menu.tsx`: The popup over the composer, while a slash line is being typed.
- `stream-turn.ts`: Reading one turn's stream in the browser.
- `studio-events.ts`: The studio folder, pushed to the window as it changes.
- `surface.ts`: What the agent knows about the screen it is standing on.
- `transcript-store.ts`: Keeping the conversation across a navigation.
- `turn-limit.ts`: How often this window may spend money.
- `turn-stream.ts`: One agent turn, streamed, with the Gate carried both ways.
- `turn.ts`: What a line in the agent's transcript is.
- `use-agent-chat.ts`: The window's conversation: what has been said, what is being said, and what is being asked.
- `use-floating.ts`: Moving and resizing the agent panel, and remembering where it was left.
- `use-kit-commands.ts`: The window's end of Kit's command layer: the catalog, the runner, and the argument completer.
- `use-mentions.ts`: Pointing the agent at a piece from the composer.
- `use-queue.ts`: What you typed while the agent was still answering.
- `use-slash.ts`: The composer's half of the slash popup: what is open, what is highlighted, and which keys are its.
- `use-studio-changes.ts`: One shell-level SSE owner; piece changes land in the injected summary store.
- `window-session.ts`: The window's own saved session, stamped per turn.

- `import-sheet.tsx`: The import sheet's own presentation: receipt/skip/report cards and the ImportOverlay dialog that arranges them.
- `import-triage.ts`: Pure import-triage logic: sorting a drop before the wire, duplicate marking (shelf and within-drop), default-check selection, failure grouping, and receipt annotation.
- `index.tsx`: Library room: shelves, collection filters, import receipts and batch Send to the Workbench.
- `lasso.tsx`: The dragged selection box, drawn on document.body.
- `lore-shelf-ops.ts`: Lore shelf side-effects: meta load (enabled, counts, search keys) + enable/duplicate.
- `lore-workshop-dialog.tsx`: Lore workshop dialog: split/merge dual-list (vs-lore-workshop).
- `new-in-deck-button.tsx`: The "New <kind>" button on an empty deck's ghost shelf.
- `new-lorebook.ts`: Create a blank lorebook in the studio and return a summary for the workbench.
- `new-persona.ts`: Create a blank persona in the studio and return a summary for the workbench.
- `new-piece.ts`: Shared factory behind the five "create a blank piece" library actions: save the empty body, hand back the summary the shelf and workbench share.
- `new-preset.ts`: Create a blank preset in the studio and return a summary for the workbench.
- `new-quickreply.ts`: Create a blank quick-reply set and return the shared Workbench summary.
- `new-regex-set.ts`: Create a blank regex set in the studio and return a summary for the workbench.
- `overlay.tsx`: Library overlay: live deck counts and recent arrivals from the canonical summary store.
- `peek-queue.ts`: Card close-ups on demand, a few at a time.
- `persona-shelf-ops.ts`: Persona shelf ops + meta loader (P4, the regex-shelf-ops pattern): the Library reads each persona body once per listing to compute the card facts (brief, pronouns, section count, lorebook chip).
- `piece-peek.ts`: What a piece says about itself: the peek fetch behind the close-up views and the shelf's metadata facts (rough tokens, content tags, filled card fields, the source-format chip label).
- `piece-preview.tsx`: Shared authored words and carried metadata across compact and close-up Library views.
- `rail.tsx`: The Library rail: RoleCall's collection sidebar, ported.
- `rc-cards.tsx`: The Library's cards, ported from RoleCall's own library rows: ContentCharacterCard (the tall portrait card) for characters and personas, ContentPresetCard for presets, ContentRegexCard for regex sets, and the vertical...
- `rc-styles.ts`: The RoleCall port's CSS: the rail, the top bar, the Carousel's section headers and rows, and the RC cards.
- `regex-shelf-ops.ts`: Regex shelf side-effects: per-set meta load (enabled, rule counts, computed "does what") plus the set-level ops a shelf card fires (toggle on/off, duplicate, split, merge).
- `regex-workshop-dialog.tsx`: Regex workshop dialog: split one set / merge two, via the shared TransferBench in an InkDialog.
- `search-bar.tsx`: The Library's search chrome: the box that sits in the deck bar, and the notice that stands on the shelf when a search matched nothing here.
- `search-core.ts`: Library-facing compatibility exports; parser and ranking authority lives in Node-compatible Core.
- `search-debounce.ts`: F3: debounce the Library's expensive derivation (parse the query, then walk every piece in the studio) 150 ms behind the search box's own text, which stays instant - the same 150 ms shape already used by the lore entr...
- `search-runner.ts`: Reads bounded search pages for the existing all-deck Library presentation.
- `select-core.ts`: Picking pieces off a shelf: one click, a range, a held modifier, or a box dragged over them.
- `send-bar.tsx`: The staging action bar: only real when pieces are picked, and ONE Send commits the whole set.
- `send-pieces.ts`: Where a batch of staged pieces actually goes.
- `set-menu.ts`: The lorebook and regex-set tools in the card's own menu (right-click, or its "..." button).
- `shelves.tsx`: The Library's shelves, laid out as RoleCall's /library: one numbered section per deck, each a row of RC cards that scrolls sideways (Carousel) or wraps (Grid), RC's LibrarySectionHeader and HorizontalAssetRow ported.
- `styles.ts`: Library app chrome CSS (injected <style> string).
- `trash-panel.tsx`: The Library's Trash: RoleCall's Trash panel, ported (design/wireframes/rc-port/trash.html), holding cut scenes and deleted takes beside the pieces (rc-port/scene-trash.html), filtered with RoleCall's segmented toggle.
- `use-library-piece-updates.ts`: Reflects accepted summary receipts without re-reading the inventory or unrelated bodies.
- `use-marquee.ts`: Dragging a box over the shelf, the way a file manager does.
- `use-picking.ts`: The shelf's selection, held in one place.
- `use-search-results.ts`: Keeps remote search results tied to the exact query and visible library snapshot.
- `variant-styles.ts`: The Library's surface-variant stylesheets (library.grid, library.card, library.decks), imported for their side effect.
- `view-contract.ts`: The deck-view contract (CONTRACT V2) - how a deck is presented (grid / showcase / list, user-sized art) is a DROP-IN MODULE.

## src/ui/apps/library/views/

- `grid.tsx`: Deck view: GRID - the workhorse.
- `list.tsx`: Deck view: LIST - dense rows for big collections: small art thumb, name, kind tag, workbench state.
- `persona-shelf.tsx`: Deck view: PERSONA SHELF (design/vs-persona-editor.html wire 2) - persona cards: portrait (or monogram) cover with the gilt Default badge, name, the BRIEF as the card line (its one honest home), and the pronouns · sec...
- `regex-shelf.tsx`: Deck view: REGEX SHELF (vs-regex-shelf-gallery wire 1) - regex sets as spine-stripe cards.
- `registry.ts`: Deck-view registry - the ONE seam for drop-in views.
- `shelf.tsx`: Deck view: SHELF - lorebook spine cards (vs-lore-shelf).
- `showcase.tsx`: Deck view: SHOWCASE - one card at a time, properly.

## src/ui/apps/macro-lab/

- `bible-pane.tsx`: The macro bible: every macro this platform publishes, and clicking one puts it in your text.
- `engine-pane.tsx`: The engine box: what the platform's own macro engine did with the text.
- `index.tsx`: Macro Lab - write macro text, see what it means and what it really resolves to.
- `lab-core.ts`: The Macro Lab's reading half: what the catalogs know about a piece of macro text.
- `ops-pane.tsx`: The operations table: one row per thing a macro can DO, one column per platform.
- `overlay.tsx`: Macro Lab overlay: real engine availability, without running any draft or imported macro.
- `reading-pane.tsx`: The reading box: what the catalogs know about the text on the left.

## src/ui/apps/press/

- `agent-surface.ts`: What the Press tells the agent window about itself.
- `index.tsx`: The Press - the locked vs-press-room-1 room, staging grammar: no studio browser in here.
- `overlay.tsx`: Press overlay: the actual staged export queue, without creating a second export path.
- `press-bundles.ts`: Bundle grouping for the Press queue (the locked vs-press-room-1 wire): a staged piece with LINKS is a bundle - its linked pieces ride with it automatically, droppable per run.
- `press-core.ts`: The Press's functional core (the Job Sheet, vs-press option 1): platform grouping, run planning, filename minting, payload bytes, and the summary line.
- `readiness-core.ts`: Press readiness core (the locked vs-press-room-1 wire): per piece x platform, which of the platform's carried canonical paths are FILLED on the body, which are empty, folded to the row's verdict.
- `variant-styles.ts`: The Press's app.room variant stylesheets, imported for their side effect and keyed on the root data-surface attribute.

## src/ui/apps/scenes/loadout/

- `fake-loadout-api.ts`: A stand-in for the part of the scenes api the loadout engine uses (tests only): the scene's settings in memory, every call logged in order, receipts timed by a clock the test owns, and a way to make any one call fail.
- `fake-scenes-store.ts`: A stand-in for the scenes api that holds one scene and one loadout library in memory (tests only): the engine's fake (fake-loadout-api.ts) for the settings and receipts, with each setter answering the chat as the stor...
- `loadout-banner.tsx`: The receipt banner (RoleCall's LoadoutReceiptBanner.tsx): the latest apply on this scene while its 15 minutes are open, with an Undo.
- `loadout-bar.tsx`: The Booth's loadout bar (RoleCall's QuickPlayPanel.tsx:1521-1533): the receipt banner, Change setup, and Save this scene as a loadout.
- `loadout-capture.ts`: Captures a loadout from a scene: for each ticked live panel, the scene's value now, or null when the scene has none (which a later apply reads as "clear it").
- `loadout-patch.ts`: Carries a run's setter answers to the open scene.
- `loadout-plan.ts`: Plans an apply, pure: from a loadout and the scene's live settings, one row per panel (what will change, what stays, what cannot be done) and the units to write in the fixed order.
- `loadout-review.tsx`: The review dialog (RoleCall's LoadoutApplyReviewDialog.tsx): what applying a loadout would change before it does.
- `loadout-run.ts`: Applies a loadout to a scene: plan from the settings the store holds now, save the receipt, then write each unit through its own setter in the fixed order.
- `loadout-save-core.ts`: What the save dialog offers (RoleCall's LoadoutSaveButton.tsx:214-274): the ten live panels in RoleCall's groups, which of them the scene holds something in (those start ticked), the one-word summary beside each, and...
- `loadout-save-dialog.tsx`: The save dialog (RoleCall's LoadoutSaveButton.tsx:395-560): a name, New loadout or Update existing, and "What to include", the ten live panels in RoleCall's groups with All current / Writing only / None.
- `loadout-style.ts`: The loadout bar's styles.
- `loadout-switcher.tsx`: The "Change the setup" sheet (RoleCall's SceneLoadoutSwitcher.tsx): every saved loadout with Apply (at once) and Review changes (the plan first), the one that is active marked by its id (RoleCall matches by name), and...
- `loadout-text.ts`: The words the loadout surfaces say, and the clock rules behind them (RoleCall's LoadoutReceiptBanner.tsx, receipt.ts `UNDO_MS`): what the receipt banner reads in its three states, the Undo notice, and the 15 minute wi...
- `loadout-undo.ts`: Undoes an apply, in reverse order and through the same setter that wrote each unit.
- `loadout-units.ts`: The ten units a loadout writes through: what the scene holds for each now, the one way to compare two values of a unit, and the one setter call that writes it.
- `use-scene-loadouts.ts`: What the Booth's loadout bar reads: the loadout library and this scene's latest open receipt, both from the library file (no browser storage, no window events), so the banner survives leaving the scene and a reload.

- `chat-thread.ts`: scene.layout chat-thread (GirliePop): a centered thread, a sticker avatar at the foot of each turn, flat backdrop.
- `manuscript.ts`: scene.layout manuscript (Paper): one narrow page, a ruled left margin, the portrait as a small plate in the margin.
- `stage-column.ts`: scene.layout stage-column (Theater): one prose column on the stage, a small portrait beside each speaker, hairline rules between turns.
- `vn-stage.ts`: scene.layout vn-stage (Encore): a framed portrait slot beside each box, dense grid, hatch backdrop (design/system scene variant B).

## src/ui/apps/scenes/variants/scene.message/

- `bubble.ts`: scene.message bubble (GirliePop): a tinted slab bubble with a stamp shadow, a small name label, the turn number as a sticker.
- `prose.ts`: scene.message prose (Theater): no box, the speaker in small caps over open prose, actions as a quiet line under the text.
- `screenplay.ts`: scene.message screenplay (Paper): the speaker in capitals, dialogue in a centered column, the turn number in the margin as a scene mark.
- `vn-box.ts`: scene.message vn-box (Encore): a bracketed dialogue box, a name plate on its top edge, the turn number as a slot tag, a hard-stepped reveal on the newest reply.

## src/ui/apps/scenes/variants/scene.reasoning/

- `footnote.ts`: scene.reasoning footnote (Paper): no frame and no fill; a hairline rule and a numeral like notes at the foot of a page, a double rule for native and a dashed numeral for tags.
- `program-note.ts`: scene.reasoning program-note (Theater): a thin margin bar down the left edge, flush for native and indented and dashed for tags, like a program note.

- `slot-tab.ts`: scene.reasoning slot-tab (Encore): a numbered label tab breaks the top edge of a thin frame, like a save slot: 01 native, 02 tags.

## src/ui/apps/scenes/variants/scene.swipes/

- `key-chips.ts`: scene.swipes key-chips (Encore): the arrows as key caps (through the button family's data-control hook), the count as a mono slot readout.
- `margin-numbers.ts`: scene.swipes margin-numbers (Paper): the version control set as a footnote in typewriter mono, aligned to the page margin.

## src/ui/apps/scenes/variants/scene.wings/

- `agent-surface.ts`: What the Workbench tells the agent window about itself.

- `editor-core.ts`: Character editor core - the pure logic under the Workbench's writable pane, transcribed from RoleCall's CharacterEditorBento and re-homed onto the canonical character body.
- `editor-derive.ts`: Pure derivations for the character editor - tolerant readers and rough estimates over a draft body, lifted out of Editor.tsx so they get a tested home the component body never gave them.
- `editor-key-owner.ts`: A split editor owns keys from its own frame; only the primary pane handles workspace-level shortcuts.
- `editor-save.ts`: Character-editor save and reconciliation orchestration.
- `editor-styles.ts`: Merged Editor CSS modules (chrome/fields/bento/playbill/quiz/composites).
- `Editor.tsx`: Character editor shell: draft/save/lens state and presenter choice.
- `fields.ts`: Field-module registry - the HUB of the editor's flow system.
- `framed.tsx`: Whether a theme's editor.frame variant is in charge of the layout; "core" keeps each editor's own arrangement.
- `htmldoc-editor.tsx`: HtmlDocEditor - write a drawing on the left, watch it draw on the right.
- `htmldoc-split.ts`: Where the divider sits between the source and the drawing - pure, because a drag is not testable here and the arithmetic is the part that can be wrong.
- `index.tsx`: The Workbench app - home: the IDE (CONTRACT V2 port of the deleted vanilla index.ts).
- `LorebookEditor.tsx`: LorebookEditor owns lorebook editing, history, TOC search, and revision-checked saves.
- `native-render.tsx`: Native-field rendering for twin (original-bound) fields.
- `overlay.tsx`: Workbench overlay: open editors, unsaved markers and the existing split-pane action.
- `pack-editor-core.ts`: Pack editor document projection.
- `PackEditor.tsx`: PackEditor - open a library pack entity as a "folder": name, brief, full sprite grid.
- `PersonaEditor.tsx`: PersonaEditor mount point - re-exports the real editor (persona/persona-editor.tsx, the RC PersonaPanel port per design/vs-persona-editor.html).
- `PresetEditor.tsx`: PresetEditor mount point - re-exports the real editor (preset/preset-editor.tsx, the hybrid wire per design/vs-preset-hybrid.html).
- `QuickReplyEditor.tsx`: Stable Workbench mount point for the quick-reply set editor.
- `recents-core.ts`: Recents ranking - pure logic for the Workbench's "bring one up" rail.
- `RegexSetEditor.tsx`: RegexSetEditor - the workbench mount point for a regex set.
- `tag-category-style.tsx`: Tag-category presentation: the UI's read of the pure `categorizeTag` result (core/tag-taxonomy) - a base color + a small line glyph per category, for the editor's tag chips.
- `use-character-history.ts`: Character composite history application and keyboard binding.
- `use-character-pack-catalog.ts`: Loads the light character/pack picker rows only while SpritePackDialog is open.
- `use-character-preferences.ts`: Character editor preferences shared by its header and lens strip.
- `use-editor-guards.ts`: Shared Ctrl+S, window-close, autosave, and shell dirty-state wiring for every Workbench editor.
- `use-reseed.ts`: Taking a freshly re-read piece into an editor that is already open.
- `use-variants.ts`: useVariants - the portrait card's variant state.
- `variants-art.ts`: Per-variant art for the variant strip.
- `variants-style.ts`: Every editor.frame and editor.fields variant's stylesheet (variants/<surface>/<variant>.ts), mounted once by the Workbench room.

## src/ui/apps/workbench/nodes/

- `Board.tsx`: The Board layer: free notes and frames on the canvas.
- `canvas-state.ts`: The Nodes view's persisted canvas (pure): parse the prefs record once at the boundary, serialize it back, the drag edits (nodes, board items, pinned notes) and the deterministic tidy layout.
- `EngineNodes.tsx`: The two engine steps on the canvas (dashed, not pieces): the Lore pass, and the Request with its scene picker and Preview.
- `geometry.ts`: Canvas geometry (pure): node sizes and port anchors derived from fixed row heights, rectangle hit tests, and fit-to-view.
- `Inspector.tsx`: The canvas inspector: facts and actions for the selected node, wire or board item.
- `link-write.ts`: The Nodes view's one write: add or remove a stored reference on a piece.
- `links.ts`: The Nodes view's link table and graph (pure): which stored reference fields become ports and wires, the graph derived from the open pieces' bodies, and the one body edit that adds or removes a link.
- `NodeCanvas.tsx`: The pan/zoom canvas: draws nodes, wires and the board, and owns every pointer gesture (pan, zoom, node and board drags, wire drags from a port, Delete on a selected wire).
- `NodesView.tsx`: The Workbench's Nodes view: the open pieces as nodes wired by their stored references, a board of notes and frames, and the head row (view switch, layers, Add, Tidy up, Fit).
- `PieceNode.tsx`: One piece on the canvas: a kind-colored card with portrait or initial, name, a meta line and its link ports (outgoing for characters, personas and presets; "Linked by" for what they point at).
- `pipeline.ts`: The Nodes view's Pipeline layer (pure): a preset's blocks in build order (off ones kept and marked), what feeds each marker slot, the lore pass counts, the dry-run request, the derived (non-editable) wires, and the ge...
- `PipelineLayer.tsx`: The Pipeline layer drawn: each preset opened into its blocks, the Lore pass and the Request, and the derived wires between them (solid: a piece or step feeding a slot; dashed: a lorebook feeding the lore pass).
- `PipelineNode.tsx`: A preset opened up into its blocks in build order.
- `preset-write.ts`: The Pipeline layer's writes: switch a block on or off, or move one, as a single preset patch built from the preset editor's own helpers.
- `use-canvas-state.ts`: The canvas state hook: reads ctx.prefs once, applies edits in React state, and saves back to prefs 400 ms after the last change through useDebouncedSave (which also flushes when the view closes), only when the seriali...
- `use-graph.ts`: The Nodes view's live graph: reads the stored references of every open piece that has link ports, re-reads when the library reports a new revision (or after a write here), and derives nodes and wires from them.
- `use-pipeline.ts`: The Pipeline layer's data: the scene list, the picked scene's cast, the context character's and persona's bodies and lorebooks (read only when they are not already on the canvas), and the scene's request forecast.
- `ViewSwitch.tsx`: The Workbench's Desk / Nodes switch, rendered in the editor's top-right slot and in the Nodes head row.

## src/ui/remote/

- `aux-download.ts`: The aux-package download: fetching the remote-access helper binary on demand, verified against a hash baked into this build.
- `aux-install.ts`: Putting a verified helper binary on disk - the imperative half of the aux download.
- `lan-host.ts`: LAN remote-access host state: the connect code (argon2-hashed), per-IP attempt throttling, and the device session/approval lifecycle.
- `lan-manager.ts`: LAN remote-access manager: owns the LanHost (connect code + approvals) and the LAN listener lifecycle, and exposes the host-side controls (enable/disable, approve/deny/kick, status).
- `lan-server.ts`: The LAN remote-access listener: a LAN-bound Bun.serve with a self-signed cert that gates every device through the connect-code + host-approval flow (LanHost) before serving the app.
- `sidecar-manager.ts`: Remote-access sidecar manager: the imperative shell that owns the Go sidecar process.
- `sidecar-pins.ts`: The remote-access helper, pinned like a dependency.
- `sidecar-status.ts`: Remote-access sidecar status: the pure core that turns the Go sidecar's control channel (one JSON status object per line on its stdout) into the app-facing RemoteState the UI renders and the API returns.

- `AppRooms.tsx`: Retain workspace, overlay and draft owners; route each app's one live instance to its presentation host.
- `AppSurface.tsx`: One mounted app tree, moved between the workspace and its floating overlay without resetting drafts.
- `boot-error.ts`: Turns shell boot failures into plain, actionable recovery instructions.
- `BootErrorScreen.tsx`: Studio boot-failure screen with direct recovery and Remote Access setup links.
- `ClosePieceDialog.tsx`: Shell-owned confirmation before closing an editor that still has unsaved changes.
- `dock-core.ts`: Pure dock roster: every available app is reachable, with utility apps separated at the end.
- `Dock.tsx`: RoleCall's persistent bottom-dock interaction, backed by Bunraku's discovered apps and host navigation.
- `DropVeil.tsx`: DropVeil - app-wide file-drop feedback.
- `error-boundary.tsx`: The last net under the whole shell.
- `FollowDialog.tsx`: FollowDialog - the shell's "N items were sent to the Workbench.
- `library-gate.tsx`: The library gate: what the window shows before the Studio (rule 12).
- `Menu.tsx`: Menu - the one right-click renderer for the whole app (transcribed 1:1 from src/ui/_shared/context-menu.ts's `show`/document-listener behavior, store-driven per CONTRACT V2).
- `menus.ts`: The shell context-menu system (ported from _shared/context-menu.ts): target registry, per-type providers, and the ref-callback hook.
- `NavigationOverlay.tsx`: Temporary game-style app launcher over the mounted workspace, using host-owned navigation and import.
- `protected-watchdog.ts`: The protected-layer watchdog.
- `settings-request-queue.ts`: Serialize settings writes and let appearance reads wait for the latest queued write.
- `status-bar-label.ts`: Name the appearance the host verified, never the previous three-value setting shape.
- `StatusBar.tsx`: Contextual status beside the top chrome: real messages, Browser Studio's warning and approved theme slots.
- `store-boot-status.ts`: The boot-status slice of the shell store (store.ts, CONTRACT V2): whether the studio's piece list has confirmed a restored bench yet (`restoringWorkspace`), its final un-retryable failure if any (`bootListFailed`), an...
- `store-core.ts`: Shell store pure helpers - the Workbench recents map's parse + merge (functional core for shell/store.ts).
- `store-dev-update.ts`: Source updates and stale-session recovery offers are transient shell state until the person chooses to reload.
- `store-mod-guard.ts`: Wires the protected-layer watchdog to the shell store: a mod that hides or covers a protected prompt is dropped at once, marked broken on the host (a later enable needs fresh approval), and the page repainted from the...
- `store-navigation.ts`: Workspace and temporary overlay destinations share app implementations, never navigation state.
- `store.ts`: Own serializable shell state and actions; app-contract selectors and App.tsx adapt this store.
- `StudioUpdate.tsx`: One explicit reload action for pending source updates and server restarts, shared by top bar and overlay.
- `TabStrip.tsx`: Open-piece tabs above the canvas.
- `theme-asset-urls.ts`: Materialize verified package images and fonts for static browser mode, where CSS cannot call the API shim.
- `theme-mod-baseline.ts`: Preserve CSS motion while the watchdog synchronously samples the host-only appearance.
- `theme-mod.ts`: Applies the checked styles and slots of the effective theme, and guards the protected layer while they are on.
- `theme-tokens.ts`: Project a validated theme appearance onto the design-system custom properties (--bk-*, from design/system/tokens).
- `theme.ts`: Paint only the verified effective theme descriptor; the core fallback is not a package.
- `title-art.tsx`: The newest scene's backdrop art for a title variant, or nothing when there is no scene (or no piece store).
- `title-core.ts`: title-core - the pure boot decision for the attract title screen (Scenes P4): whether a fresh launch should open on the attract screen before anything else, or boot should go straight to wherever it already would.
- `TitleScreen.tsx`: The title screen.
- `use-app-keys.ts`: The one listener that turns a chord into a room change - the imperative half of app-keys.ts.
- `use-boot.ts`: use-boot - the shell's boot sequence (CONTRACT V2, perf plan Phase 2 / R4), split out of App.tsx.
- `use-shell-menus.ts`: useShellMenus - registers the shell-owned context-menu providers (entity/app/shell), identical in every room.
- `use-theme-events.ts`: Keep each visible shell tab aligned with host-authorized appearance changes.
- `use-title.ts`: The title screen's behavior, shared by every shell.title variant: the newest scene for "continue", the five ways out (each dismisses once into its destination), and the Enter key.
- `use-workspace.ts`: Remembering the bench across a restart, and putting it back.
- `variant-styles.ts`: The shell's surface-variant stylesheets, imported for their side effect.
- `workspace-core.ts`: What the bench looked like, so it can look like that again.

## src/ui/shell/variants/shell.title/

- `attract.tsx`: shell.title attract: an arcade attract screen.
- `sticker-splash.tsx`: shell.title sticker-splash: a scrapbook page.
- `title-page.tsx`: shell.title title-page: the opening leaf of a book.

## src/ui/spotify/

- `client.ts`: Every byte this app sends to Spotify goes through the two fetches built here, each locked to ONE host by the egress gate's guardedFetch (which also refuses a redirect to anywhere else): `accounts.spotify.com` for the...
- `link.ts`: The Spotify link: the person's own Client ID, the PKCE sign-in, and playback control, all local.
- `pkce.ts`: The PKCE half of the Spotify sign-in (RFC 7636): verifier, S256 challenge, state, the loopback redirect and the authorize URL.
- `routes.ts`: Spotify's two doors on the local server.

## src/ui/switch/

- `git-runner.ts`: Git operations for a source-checkout version switch, behind an injected interface so the source engine's orchestration (source-engine.ts) is unit-tested with a fake and this thin real edge stays trivial.
- `manager.ts`: The switch manager: the imperative shell that runs ONE version switch at a time.
- `packaged-engine.ts`: Packaged-build switch engine.
- `routes.ts`: The version-switch routes (host-only; /api/updates/* is refused to any remote/LAN device upstream).
- `setup.ts`: Boot wiring for version switching: build the SwitchManager the trusted handler drives.
- `source-engine.ts`: Source-checkout switch engine: move a `git`-run Bunraku to a release tag by checking it out and reinstalling.

## src/ui/theme/

- `fonts.ts`: The house fonts the app ships (src/ui/theme/fonts, each under the SIL Open Font License with its OFL-<font>.txt beside it).

## src/ui/tours/docs/

- `index.ts`: First-open walkthrough for finding, reading, and navigating the in-app docs corpus.

## src/ui/tours/library/

- `index.ts`: The Library tour - the second drop-in, proving the framework generalizes: another folder, another Tour, discovered and mounted by the same engine with zero new wiring.

## src/ui/tours/press/

- `index.ts`: The Press tour - the staging-grammar room (vs-press-room-1).

## src/ui/tours/settings/

- `index.ts`: The Settings tour - same drop-in chassis as the workbench/library tours, pure data.

## src/ui/tours/

- `tour-contract.ts`: The tour contract - folders-as-schema for in-app tutorials, mirroring setup/step-contract.ts.
- `tour-core.ts`: Pure sequencing logic for the tour engine (the functional core the TourGuide shell wraps).

## src/ui/tours/workbench-lorebook/

- `index.ts`: The lorebook binder walkthrough - served by the workbench "?" when a lorebook is the active piece.

## src/ui/tours/workbench-pack/

- `index.ts`: The pack folder walkthrough - served by the workbench "?" when a pack is the active piece.

## src/ui/tours/workbench-persona/

- `index.ts`: The persona editor walkthrough - served by the workbench "?" when a persona is the active piece.

## src/ui/tours/workbench-preset/

- `index.ts`: The preset editor walkthrough - served by the workbench "?" when a preset is the active piece.

## src/ui/tours/workbench-regex/

- `index.ts`: The regex set walkthrough - served by the workbench "?" when a regex set is the active piece.

## src/ui/tours/workbench/

- `index.ts`: The Workbench tour - the first drop-in over the tutorial framework, and the proof of the pattern: a folder with an index that default-exports a Tour, discovered by the same loader apps and setup steps use.
